Making distributed learning faster and fairer 130

When machine learning training spreads across many computers with different data, how do you keep progress fast and balanced? Papers tackle communication costs, unequal data distribution, and coordination.

Handling uneven data across workers

13

When different machines have different types of data (called non-IID data), the learning goes slower and less accurately; these papers find ways to make it work anyway.

ICLR 2026

Mitigating Non-IID Drift in Zeroth-Order Federated LLM Fine-Tuning with Transferable Sparsity

Problem. Federated LLM fine-tuning faces massive memory and communication challenges due to parameter scale, while non-IID data across clients further complicates convergence.
Approach. Introduces Meerkat, a sparse zeroth-order optimization method using transferable static parameter subsets with high-frequency communication and virtual path mechanisms to detect extreme Non-IID clients.
Contribution. Enables cost-effective federated LLM fine-tuning with superior performance compared to full-parameter zeroth-order baselines and improved handling of data heterogeneity.
zeroth-order optimizationsparsityfederated learningnon-iid handling

ICLR 2026

Clipped Gradient Methods for Nonsmooth Convex Optimization under Heavy-Tailed Noise: A Refined Analysis

Problem. Optimization under heavy-tailed noise (bounded p-th moment, p∈(1,2]) requires better convergence rates than existing Clipped SGD analyses provide.
Approach. Provides refined analysis of Clipped SGD with improved rates utilizing Freedman's inequality and introducing generalized effective dimension concept.
Contribution. Offers faster convergence rates: O(σₗdₑff^(-1/2p)ln^(1-1/p)(1/δ)T^(1/p-1)) for nonsmooth convex and O(σₗ²dₑff^(-1/p)ln^(2-2/p)(1/δ)T^(2/p-2)) for strongly convex problems.
gradient clippingheavy-tailed noiseconvergence analysis
ICLR 2026

Ringleader ASGD: The First Asynchronous SGD with Optimal Time Complexity under Data Heterogeneity

Problem. Asynchronous SGD struggles with heterogeneous data/computation: unrealistic similarity assumptions and suboptimal performance under heterogeneous speeds.
Approach. Ringleader ASGD attains optimal parallel first-order stochastic method rates without similarity assumptions, under arbitrary/time-varying worker speeds.
Contribution. First asynchronous SGD achieving theoretical lower bounds in smooth nonconvex regime with optimal time complexity under data heterogeneity.
asynchronous stochastic gradient descentdistributed optimizationfederated learning
ICLR 2026

Non-Convex Federated Optimization under Cost-Aware Client Selection

Problem. Existing federated optimization methods do not distinguish between client-selection strategies that incur different communication costs in practice, making it difficult to compare algorithms fairly.
Approach. A new federated optimization model quantifies communication and local computation complexities for different client-selection strategies, proposing an algorithm with a carefully constructed gradient estimator based on SAGA and a Recursive-Gradient technique to improve error bounds.
Contribution. The proposed algorithm achieves the best-known communication and local complexities among existing federated optimization methods for non-convex optimization.
variance reductionfederated learninggradient estimationSAGA
ICLR 2026

Unified Analyses for Hierarchical Federated Learning: Topology Selection under Data Heterogeneity

Problem. Hierarchical federated learning topology selection remains open, as optimal configurations depend on complex interactions between data heterogeneity, network conditions, and client participation patterns.
Approach. Establishes a unified convergence framework analyzing all four HFL topologies (Star-Star, Star-Ring, Ring-Star, Ring-Ring) under non-convex objectives with varying intra/inter-group data heterogeneity and full/partial client participation.
Contribution. Reveals three fundamental topology selection principles: top-tier aggregation dominance, dependency on client grouping, and inter-group heterogeneity primacy, validated on CIFAR-10/CINIC-10/Fashion-MNIST/SST-2.
federated learningconvergence analysistopology selectionheterogeneous data handling
ICML 2026

FedPDG: Prediction Discrepancy–Guided Data Generation for Heterogeneous Federated Learning

Problem. Existing federated learning with synthetic data generation assumes balanced global distributions, but real distributions are imbalanced and unknown due to privacy constraints.
Approach. FedPDG uses model performance discrepancy between local and global models to identify lacking regions and adapts diffusion via preference optimization for better distribution alignment.
Contribution. FedPDG outperforms state-of-the-art methods, achieving up to 3.82% accuracy improvement.
performance discrepancypreference optimizationsynthetic data generation
ICML 2026

Trustworthy Federated Label Distribution Learning under Annotation Quality Disparity

Problem. Federated Label Distribution Learning fails when clients have heterogeneous annotation quality, as standard aggregation treats all clients equally despite reliability differences.
Approach. Proposes FedQual with quality-adaptive training guided by global semantic anchors and reliability-aware aggregation that reweights by effective reliable information.
Contribution. Theoretical guarantee showing client-specific calibration outperforms uniform calibration; superior performance on four constructed Fed-LDL benchmarks.
federated learninglabel distributionquality-aware aggregation
ICML 2026

Last-iterate Convergence of ADMM on Multi-affine Quadratic Equality Constrained Problem

Problem. Multi-affine quadratic equality constrained problems are generally non-convex with convexity only when all variables except one are fixed, making optimization difficult.
Approach. Proves alternating direction method of multipliers converges; shows linear convergence when non-convexity degree remains bounded.
Contribution. Establishes convergence guarantees for ADMM on non-convex multi-affine problems with applications in robotics and neural network training.
alternating direction method of multipliersnon-convex optimization
ICML 2026

On the Interaction of Batch Noise, Adaptivity, and Compression, under $(L_0,L_1)$-Smoothness: An SDE Approach

Problem. The joint effects of stochastic gradient noise, communication compression, and adaptive updates in distributed optimization remain poorly understood under realistic (L_0, L_1)-smoothness assumptions.
Approach. The paper develops a unified theoretical framework using first-order SDEs incorporating curvature-dependent drift terms to capture fine-grained relationships between learning rates, gradient noise, compression, and landscape geometry.
Contribution. Shows that normalizing updates emerges as a natural condition for stability under heavy-tailed and affine-variance noise, while DSignSGD converges under heavy-tailed noise with standard learning rates.
stochastic gradient descentcommunication compressionstochastic differential equations
ICML 2026

Forward-KL Convergence of Time-Inhomogeneous Langevin Diffusions

Problem. Time-dependent drifts in practical samplers improve exploration but non-asymptotic convergence analysis under annealing schedules remains underdeveloped.
Approach. Unified convergence analysis for Langevin diffusions and Euler-Maruyama discretizations in forward-KL divergence under abstract time-dependent drift conditions.
Contribution. Provides non-asymptotic bounds for continuous and discrete samplers covering practical annealing schemes like geometric tempering.
langevin diffusionstime-inhomogeneous samplingconvergence analysis
ICML 2026

FedSSM: State Space Model-based Proactive Inference for Heterogeneous Multimodal Federated Learning

Problem. Multimodal Federated Learning faces client selection challenges under compounded modality, distribution, and quantity heterogeneity; reactive methods miss how decisions influence optimization trajectories.
Approach. FedSSM reconceptualizes client selection as proactive decision-making via decision-aware state space models predicting training dynamics; surprise signal governs adaptive budgets and exploration-exploitation via counterfactual reasoning.
Contribution. FedSSM achieves 2.5-4.5% accuracy improvements over state-of-the-art while reducing communication rounds by over 30%.
federated learningstate space modelsclient selectionmultimodal learning
ICML 2026

BESplit: Bias-Compensated Split Federated Learning with Evidential Aggregation

Problem. Split federated learning suffers from biased optimization and unstable convergence under non-IID data because client information coordination lacks awareness of split architecture.
Approach. BESplit exploits split architecture structure using Evidential Aggregation for fine-grained reweighting, Bias-Compensated Collaboration aligning split-layer representations via complementary pairing, and Dual-Teacher Distillation synchronizing knowledge.
Contribution. Consistently outperforms state-of-the-art methods in accuracy, convergence stability, and computational efficiency under diverse non-IID settings.
federated learningevidential learningnon-iid datasplit learning
ICML 2026

FedReLa: Imbalanced Federated Learning via Re-Labeling

Problem. Federated learning suffers from degraded generalization under global class imbalance and cross-client data heterogeneity, with mismatch between local and global imbalances exacerbating performance.
Approach. Proposes FedReLa, a data-level approach using feature-dependent label re-allocator to correct biased global decision boundaries without requiring global class distribution knowledge.
Contribution. Significantly improves minority class accuracy and overall accuracy on stepwise-imbalanced and long-tailed datasets, outperforming state-of-the-art without additional communication overhead.
federated learningre-labelingclass imbalancedata heterogeneity

Cutting communication costs between machines

6

Sending updates between machines is slow and expensive; these papers find ways to compress or reduce the amount of information that needs to be sent.

ICLR 2026

Enhancing Communication Compression via Discrepancy-aware Calibration for Federated Learning

Problem. Federated learning communication overhead is critical; existing compression methods rely on magnitude heuristics ignoring output discrepancy.
Approach. Uses calibration data on clients to measure output discrepancy induced by dropping compression units as compression metric guiding selection.
Contribution. Achieves 18.9% relative accuracy improvement at 0.1 compression ratio under severe communication constraints.
communication compressiondiscrepancy-aware selectionfederated learning
ICLR 2026

DeMo: Decoupled Momentum Optimization

Problem. Scaling neural network training via synchronous data-parallelism faces a severe communication bottleneck from full-precision gradient all-reduce.
Approach. DeMo decouples local momentum updates, applies fast orthonormal transforms (e.g., DCT) with top-k sparsification, and reuses momentum buffers for error feedback.
Contribution. Reduces per-step communication by up to 100x with minimal computational overhead; 300M- and 1B-parameter models transmit 85x less data than AdamW-DDP while maintaining comparable loss and accuracy.
communication compressionmomentum decouplingsparsificationerror feedback
ICLR 2026

OrderDP: A Theoretically Guaranteed Lossless Dynamic Data Pruning Framework

Problem. Data pruning methods selecting highly informative samples induce biased gradient estimation compared to full-dataset training, but the bias's impact on performance remains ambiguous.
Approach. OrderDP randomly selects a subset then chooses top-q samples with unbiasedness established for a surrogate loss, providing theoretical convergence and generalization analyses showing how pruning affects optimal performance.
Contribution. OrderDP reduces training cost by over 40% while maintaining competitive accuracy and stable convergence with exact performance control, serving as a robust, easily adaptable tool for data-efficient learning.
data pruninggradient unbiasednessconvergence analysis
ICLR 2026

Unlocking the Potential of Weighting Methods in Federated Learning Through Communication Compression

Problem. Weighting methods in federated learning operate efficiently but fail to address the main obstacle: communication bottleneck in heterogeneous data scenarios.
Approach. Incorporates compression into the weighting scheme with convergence analysis under convexity for both exact and stochastic oracles.
Contribution. Establishes convergence guarantees while addressing communication bottleneck in federated learning with heterogeneous data.
federated learningcommunication compressionweighting methods
ICML 2026

Certificate-Guided Pruning for Stochastic Lipschitz Optimization

Problem. Black-box optimization of Lipschitz functions under noise lacks explicit optimality certificates and measurable progress guarantees.
Approach. Certificate-Guided Pruning (CGP) maintains an active set of potentially optimal points via confidence-adjusted Lipschitz envelopes, with theoretical convergence under margin conditions.
Contribution. CGP achieves sample complexity Õ(ε^-(2+α)) and demonstrates strong empirical performance across 12 benchmarks with principled stopping criteria.
certificate-guided pruninglipschitz optimizationconfidence-adjusted envelopes
ICML 2026

On the Convergence of Decentralized Stochastic Minimax Optimization Algorithm with Compressed Communication

Problem. Distributed stochastic minimax optimization algorithms suffer from prohibitive communication costs when scaling to large datasets.
Approach. The framework develops a decentralized stochastic gradient descent ascent with momentum using error feedback mechanism to balance full-precision updates and compression residuals, with novel coefficient designs to handle both primal and dual variable compression.
Contribution. The algorithm demonstrates theoretical convergence guarantees showing how the compression operator influences convergence rate while reducing communication overhead.
gradient descent ascenterror feedbackcommunication compression

Making federated learning prove its convergence

33

Researchers need to show mathematically that federated algorithms actually reach good solutions, especially when data is uneven and noisy across machines.

ICLR 2026

Newton Method Revisited: Global Convergence Rates up to $O(1/k^3)$ for Stepsize Schedules and Linesearch Procedures

Problem. The global convergence rates and practical efficiency of Newton methods for convex optimization remain underexplored, especially with modern stepsize schedules and linesearch procedures.
Approach. We propose stepsize schedules and linesearch procedures with fast global convergence guarantees up to O(1/k^3) for convex functions with Hölder continuous Hessians or third derivatives.
Contribution. We provide strong convergence guarantees for Newton method with exact linesearch, covering cases with multiple smoothness parameterizations or unknown smoothness constants.
newton methodstepsize scheduleslinesearch proceduresconvergence analysis

ICLR 2026

On the Convergence Direction of Gradient Descent

Problem. Gradient descent's long-term asymptotic directional properties remain poorly understood, limiting insights into optimization dynamics across different learning rates.
Approach. The paper proves that gradient descent trajectories either align toward a fixed direction (small learning rates) or oscillate along a specific line (large learning rates), supported by experiments on SGD and Adam.
Contribution. Proves directional convergence behavior in gradient descent and relates oscillatory convergence to Edge of Stability dynamics, providing new theoretical clarity on optimization behavior.
gradient descent analysisconvergence theorylearning rate dynamics
ICLR 2026

High-Probability Bounds for the Last Iterate of Clipped SGD

Problem. Stochastic gradient descent often fails to converge with high probability when gradient estimates have only finite moments.
Approach. Studies clipped SGD under the assumption that stochastic gradients have finite α-th moments for α ∈ (1,2].
Contribution. Proves high-probability convergence of order 1/K^((2α-2)/(3α)) on smooth objectives for the last iterate of clipped SGD.
stochastic gradient descentconvergence analysisclipping
ICLR 2026

Sobolev Gradient Ascent for Optimal Transport: Barycenter Optimization and Convergence Analysis

Problem. Computing Wasserstein barycenters is challenged by the need for computationally expensive c-concavity projection operators and lack of methods leveraging specialized geometries.
Approach. Introduces SGA algorithm tailoring vanilla dual gradient ascent to Sobolev geometry, eliminating the expensive c-concavity projection while providing global convergence guarantees.
Contribution. SGA achieves the same convergence rate as classical subgradient descent for nonsmooth convex minimization, with superior empirical performance and algorithmic/theoretical simplifications.
sobolev gradient ascentdual formulationwasserstein barycenters
ICLR 2026

Birch SGD: A Tree Graph Framework for Local and Asynchronous SGD Methods

Problem. Existing frameworks for distributed SGD lack unified analysis and intuitive foundation for designing efficient asynchronous optimization methods.
Approach. Birch SGD represents each method as weighted directed computation tree, reducing convergence analysis to studying tree geometry; designs eight new methods with optimal complexity.
Contribution. Provides unified graph-based interpretation of optimization dynamics with O((R+1)L∆/ε + σ²L∆/ε²) iteration rate showing trade-offs across methods.
distributed SGDtree graph frameworkasynchronous optimization
ICLR 2026

Beyond Softmax and Entropy: Convergence Rates of Policy Gradients with $\boldsymbol{f}$-SoftArgmax Parameterization $\&$ Coupled Regularization

Problem. Policy gradient methods with softmax parameterization suffer from ill-conditioned optimization landscapes and exponentially slow convergence, and existing fixes like preconditioning are computationally expensive.
Approach. The paper proposes replacing softmax with generalized f-softargmax policy parameterizations coupled with regularizers induced by f-divergence, which satisfies Polyak-Łojasiewicz inequality and enables efficient optimization without preconditioning.
Contribution. f-PG establishes the first explicit non-asymptotic last-iterate convergence guarantees for finite MDPs without preconditioning and achieves polynomial sample complexity, contrasting with exponential complexity of softmax parameterization.
policy gradientf-divergence regularizationsoftargmax parameterizationconvergence analysis
ICLR 2026

Tighter Performance Theory of FedExProx

Problem. Known theoretical guarantees for FedExProx on quadratic optimization are no better than vanilla gradient descent.
Approach. Develops novel analysis framework establishing tighter linear convergence rates and considering adaptive extrapolation strategies based on gradient diversity.
Contribution. Demonstrates FedExProx can provably outperform GD and extends to Polyak-Łojasiewicz conditions, outperforming previous strongly convex analysis.
federated learningoptimizationextrapolationdistributed training
ICLR 2026

Composite Optimization with Error Feedback: the Dual Averaging Approach

Problem. Error feedback for message compression fails in composite optimization with non-smooth regularizers or constraints, lacking theoretical foundation for this general setting.
Approach. Combines Dual Averaging with EControl (error feedback variant) and derives a convergence analysis template for inexact dual averaging with error feedback in composite settings.
Contribution. First strong convergence analysis for composite optimization with error feedback, enabling communication-efficient distributed machine learning with theoretical guarantees.
error feedbackdual averagingcomposite optimizationinexact methods
ICLR 2026

Understanding the Mechanisms of Fast Hyperparameter Transfer

Problem. While methods like muP empirically display fast hyperparameter transfer across model scales, a deeper conceptual understanding of mechanisms enabling this transfer is lacking.
Approach. The paper establishes a systematic framework analyzing fast HP transfer through novel decomposition of optimization trajectories, identifying one component that rapidly converges and determines optimal HPs.
Contribution. Work elucidates key mechanisms behind fast transfer in synthetic and practical settings including LLM training, proposing that rapid convergence of a specific trajectory component enables transfer.
hyperparameter transfertrajectory decompositionconvergence analysis
ICLR 2026

Derandomized Online-to-Non-convex Conversion for Stochastic Weakly Convex Optimization

Problem. Derandomizing online-to-non-convex conversion is impossible in general, but practical algorithms like SGDM use deterministic updates that lack theoretical guarantees.
Approach. Shows that online-to-non-convex conversion can be derandomized for weakly convex functions, developing a deterministic algorithm with optimal convergence rates when weak convexity parameter is O(delta^-1 epsilon^-1).
Contribution. Provides a momentum-restarted SGDM variant with optimal theoretical guarantees and empirical effectiveness on ResNet and ViT networks.
online-to-non-convex conversionweak convexitymomentum methods
ICLR 2026

Error Feedback for Muon and Friends

Problem. Recent non-Euclidean optimizers like Muon lack principled distributed frameworks and communication-efficient implementations with convergence guarantees.
Approach. EF21-Muon extends error feedback to non-Euclidean settings, supporting stochastic gradients, momentum, and bidirectional compression with rigorous convergence analysis.
Contribution. EF21-Muon achieves up to 7x communication savings with no accuracy loss compared to uncompressed Muon/Scion/Gluon across NanoGPT benchmarks.
error feedbacknon-euclidean optimizationlayer-wise linear minimizationcommunication efficiency
ICML 2026

Convergence Analysis of the Lion Optimizer in Centralized and Distributed Settings

Problem. Convergence analysis of the Lion optimizer in centralized and distributed settings is not well understood, particularly regarding communication efficiency.
Approach. Provides comprehensive convergence analysis establishing O(d^1/2 T^-1/4) rate for original Lion, proposes variance reduction variant with O(d^1/2 T^-1/3) rate, and extends to distributed settings with sign compression.
Contribution. Establishes linear speedup with respect to number of nodes and introduces communication-efficient variants with convergence guarantees.
optimizer analysisvariance reductiondistributed optimization
ICML 2026

Natural Hypergradient Descent: Algorithm Design, Convergence Analysis, and Parallel Implementation

Problem. Bilevel optimization requires computing Hessian inverses for hypergradient estimation, creating computational bottlenecks that limit practical scalability.
Approach. Exploits statistical structure of inner optimization using empirical Fisher information as Hessian surrogate in parallel optimize-and-approximate framework with synchronous updates.
Contribution. Matches state-of-the-art optimize-then-approximate sample complexity with significantly reduced computational overhead; demonstrates scalability on representative bilevel tasks.
bilevel optimizationhypergradient descentfisher information
ICML 2026

From Optimization to Generalization under Heavy-Tailed Data: The Role of Gradient Clipping

Problem. Standard SGD convergence guarantees degrade on heavy-tailed data where gradients lack finite second moments, even though empirical moments are finite once data is fixed.
Approach. Separates data sampling from optimization randomness showing heavy-tailed data induce dataset-dependent noise scaling as N^(2/alpha-1) under tail index alpha, then proves SGD with gradient clipping avoids this growth.
Contribution. Provides finite-sum convergence guarantees for SGD with clipping under heavy-tailed data and derives generalization bounds showing tail behavior at population minimizer links optimization and generalization.
gradient clippingheavy-tailed dataconvergence analysisgeneralization bounds
ICML 2026

From Muon to Gluon: Bridging Theory and Practice of LMO-based Optimizers for LLMs

Problem. Recent LMO-based optimizers like Muon show practical advantages but lack theoretical understanding due to overlooking layer-wise application and unrealistic smoothness assumptions.
Approach. Introduces Gluon, a new LMO-based framework with refined generalized smoothness model capturing layer-wise geometry, matching practical layer-wise implementation of Muon and Scion.
Contribution. Provides state-of-the-art convergence guarantees matching theoretical assumptions to practice; experiments confirm assumptions hold along optimization trajectory.
optimizationconvergence theoryneural networksgradient descent
ICML 2026

Convergence Analysis of Decentralized Hessian-/Jacobian-Free Algorithm for Nonconvex Stochastic Bilevel Optimization

Problem. Existing decentralized bi-level optimization assumes lower-level strong convexity; extending to nonconvex lower-level while avoiding artificial regularization remains open.
Approach. Proposes decentralized stochastic first-order algorithm for nonconvex lower-level satisfying Polyak-Łojasiewicz condition; uses constant single-timescale learning rate.
Contribution. First algorithm for nonconvex decentralized bi-level optimization with convergence guarantees under mild conditions; validated on hyperparameter optimization and model pruning.
polyak-lojasiewicz conditiondecentralized optimizationbi-level optimization
ICML 2026

Stochastic Gradient Methods under Heavy-Tailed Noises in Weakly Convex Optimization

Problem. Heavy-tailed gradient noise in stochastic optimization has limited theoretical analysis in weakly convex settings, and convergence guarantees under realistic noise distributions are lacking.
Approach. The paper derives convergence bounds for SGD and Clip-SGD under heavy-tailed noises, establishing in-expectation and high-probability guarantees under different assumptions on gradient noise.
Contribution. Provides high-probability convergence bounds with polynomial-logarithmic dependence on failure probability and validates findings through experiments.
stochastic gradient descentheavy-tailed noiseweakly convex optimization
ICML 2026

Rethinking Neural Network Learning Rates: A Stackelberg Perspective

Problem. Neural networks typically use uniform learning rates across layers despite empirical evidence that layer-specific learning rates accelerate training, but principled understanding of when and why remains limited.
Approach. Investigates non-uniform learning rates via Stackelberg optimization, showing smaller learning rates for body layers and larger for final layers can be interpreted as two-time-scale alternating gradient descent on a Stackelberg reformulation.
Contribution. Establishes finite-time convergence guarantees and identifies two mechanisms by which non-uniform learning rates outperform uniform ones: Stackelberg objective optimization structure and sharper local curvature.
stackelberg optimizationtwo-time-scale gradient descentlayer-specific learning ratesconvergence analysis
ICML 2026

Zeroth-Order Non-Log-Concave Sampling with Variance Reduction and Applications to Inverse Problems

Problem. Sampling from high-dimensional non-log-concave distributions with black-box access lacks practical stability and non-asymptotic convergence guarantees.
Approach. A variance-reduced zeroth-order Langevin method uses a gradient estimator that reduces variance and eliminates unfavorable dimensional dependence, with convergence theory via Fisher information and Poincaré inequalities.
Contribution. Establishes first non-asymptotic convergence guarantees for zeroth-order non-log-concave sampling and posterior sampling via score-based generative priors.
zeroth-order langevinvariance reductionfisher informationscore-based generative priors
ICML 2026

A Short and Unified Convergence Analysis of the SAG, SAGA, and IAG Algorithms

Problem. Existing convergence analyses for variance-reduced algorithms like SAG, SAGA, and IAG are disparate, relying on different proof techniques, making it difficult to understand their unified behavior and obtain the best possible rates.
Approach. Develops a single unified convergence analysis applying to all three algorithms via establishing delay bounds from sub-sampling using concentration tools, then carefully designing a novel Lyapunov function accounting for such delays.
Contribution. Provides short and modular proofs with high-probability bounds for SAG and SAGA extendable to non-convex objectives and Markovian sampling, obtaining best known rates for IAG significantly improving prior bounds.
variance reductionstochastic gradient descentconvergence analysis
ICML 2026

Why Do We Need Warm-up? A Theoretical Perspective

Problem. Learning rate warm-up is ubiquitous in deep learning but its theoretical foundations remain poorly understood.
Approach. The paper provides a principled explanation using a generalization of $(L_0, L_1)$-smoothness that bounds local curvature as a linear function of loss suboptimality and adapting learning rate in response to this condition.
Contribution. The resulting one-parameter warm-up schedule yields provably faster convergence guarantees than fixed learning rate and matches tuned linear warm-up on language and vision models.
learning rate schedulingsmoothness conditionconvergence analysiswarm-up
ICML 2026

A Tight Theory of Error Feedback Algorithms in Distributed Optimization

Problem. Gradient compression in distributed optimization degrades convergence guarantees, yet error feedback mechanisms lack unified understanding of their relative performance and convergence properties.
Approach. Provides tight convergence analyses for Error Feedback (EF) and Error Feedback 21 (EF21) methods by identifying optimal step-size choices and constructing optimal Lyapunov functions tailored to each algorithm.
Contribution. Recovers known best guarantees possible in single-agent regime with convergence analysis independent of the number of agents.
error feedbackgradient compressiondistributed optimizationconvergence analysis
ICML 2026

Convergence Rate Analysis of the AdamW-Style Shampoo: Unifying One-Sided and Two-Sided Preconditioning

Problem. Convergence rate analysis for AdamW-style Shampoo remains unclear, particularly when unifying one-sided and two-sided preconditioning.
Approach. Establishes convergence rate using nuclear norm gradient measurement, showing O(sqrt(m+n)C/K^{1/4}) for matrix parameters of size (m,n).
Contribution. Proves convergence rate analogous to optimal SGD under ideal spectral conditions with balanced dimensions.
shampoo optimizerpreconditioningconvergence analysis
ICML 2026

Local Constrained Bayesian Optimization

Problem. Bayesian optimization for high-dimensional constrained problems suffers from the curse of dimensionality and trust-region methods prone to premature shrinking under tight or complex constraints.
Approach. LCBO leverages differentiable constraint-penalized surrogates to alternate between rapid local descent and uncertainty-driven exploration, with theoretical guarantees on KKT residual convergence that scale polynomially with dimension.
Contribution. Achieves polynomial convergence rates on the KKT residual for high-dimensional problems (up to 100D) and consistently outperforms state-of-the-art baselines.
local constrained optimizationsurrogate modelskkt convergence
ICML 2026

Understanding MARS: When Scaling Momentum Provably Helps

Problem. MARS optimizer improves LLM training by scaling momentum correction terms, but existing theory does not explain why this modification outperforms unscaled variance reduction.
Approach. The work introduces gamma-similarity to capture how scaling coefficient interacts with gradient-difference structure, deriving convergence bounds showing smaller gamma values reduce similarity constants.
Contribution. Theory predicts when optimized gamma outperforms unscaled momentum-based variance reduction, validated on GPT-style LLM pretraining.
momentum-based variance reductiongamma-similarityadaptive step sizesconvergence analysis
ICML 2026

Convergence Rate of the Last Iterate of Stochastic Proximal Algorithms

Problem. Analyzing last-iterate convergence for stochastic proximal algorithms typically requires stringent bounded variance assumptions.
Approach. Analyzes proximal stochastic gradient and randomized incremental proximal methods under componentwise convexity and smoothness.
Contribution. Proves Õ(1/√T) convergence rate for last iterate under relaxed bounded variance assumptions; applies to graph-guided regularizers in federated learning.
stochastic optimizationproximal methodsconvergence analysis
ICML 2026

Distributed Direct Preference Optimization

Problem. Direct Preference Optimization (DPO) lacks convergence guarantees in distributed settings where preference data are fragmented across heterogeneous users, and theoretical behavior remains poorly understood.
Approach. Provides the first convergence and time-complexity analysis of DPO in federated and decentralized environments, modeling personalized offline RL with user-specific preference distributions and characterizing the induced global optimization landscape.
Contribution. Derives convergence rates quantifying the impact of client drift, communication frequency, and preference heterogeneity for federated DPO, and establishes convergence over general communication graphs for decentralized DPO.
distributed preference optimizationconvergence analysisfederated learningdecentralized training
ICML 2026

Improved Convergence Analysis of Topology Dependence in Decentralized SGD

Problem. The convergence behavior of Decentralized SGD is not fully understood, particularly how network topology affects convergence in heterogeneous settings.
Approach. The paper presents a tighter convergence analysis showing that all eigenvalues of the mixing matrix, not just spectral gap, affect the convergence rate.
Contribution. The novel analysis more accurately describes the effect of topology on convergence rate than prior work in both homogeneous and heterogeneous cases.
spectral analysisconvergence analysiseigenvalue characterization
ICML 2026

Stability Analysis of Sharpness-Aware Minimization

Problem. Sharpness-aware minimization can become stuck at saddle points, exhibiting convergence instability that impacts generalization in deep learning.
Approach. Uses qualitative dynamical systems theory to analyze SAM convergence near saddle points and proves that saddle points can become attractors under SAM dynamics, with worse diffusion than vanilla gradient descent.
Contribution. Demonstrates that momentum and batch-size are important for mitigating instability and achieving high generalization across optimization problems and benchmarks.
sharpness-aware minimizationdynamical systemssaddle point analysisconvergence theory
ICML 2026

Towards Understanding Adam Convergence on Highly Degenerate Polynomials

Problem. The specific class of objective functions where Adam exhibits inherent advantages and its auto-convergence properties without external schedulers remains underexplored.
Approach. Identifies class of highly degenerate polynomials where Adam converges automatically; derives theoretical conditions for local asymptotic stability on degenerate functions.
Contribution. Proves Adam achieves local linear convergence on degenerate functions, significantly outperforming sub-linear convergence of Gradient Descent; characterizes hyperparameter phase diagram.
adaptive optimizationconvergence analysisphase space characterization
ICML 2026

Learning Rate Annealing Improves Tuning Robustness in Stochastic Optimization

Problem. Learning rate tuning via grid search is costly for large-scale models, and coarse grids lead to poor hyperparameter specification.
Approach. Analyzes learning rate annealing schemes with polynomial decay (e.g., cosine schedule) in stochastic convex optimization, showing sublinear dependence on grid resolution misspecification factor.
Contribution. Demonstrates convergence rate of O(ρ^(1/(2p+1))/√T) for annealed schedules versus O(ρ/√T) for fixed stepsize, achieving increased robustness to initial learning rate misspecification.
learning rate schedulingconvergence analysishyperparameter robustness
ICML 2026

Convergence of Steepest Descent and Adam under Non-Uniform Smoothness

Problem. First-order optimization methods under non-uniform smoothness assumptions that better model practical loss landscapes lack convergence analysis for adaptive methods like RMSProp and Adam.
Approach. The paper analyzes steepest descent and adaptive variants (RMSProp, Adam, diagonal versions) under non-uniform smoothness where curvature is an affine function of objective value, establishing convergence rates under gradient domination conditions.
Contribution. Results show sign GD converges linearly and faster than GD for logistic regression on separable data, and Adam/RMSProp achieve linear convergence with constant step-size on certain neural networks.
first-order optimizationadaptive methodsconvergence analysis
ICML 2026

High-Probability Convergence Guarantees of Decentralized SGD

Problem. High-probability convergence guarantees for decentralized SGD remain poorly understood as existing works require restrictive assumptions like uniformly bounded gradients.
Approach. Study high-probability convergence of decentralized SGD under light-tailed noise using same conditions on cost function as needed for MSE convergence.
Contribution. Achieves order-optimal convergence rates for non-convex and strongly convex costs with linear speed-up in number of users under relaxed assumptions.
distributed optimizationstochastic gradient descentconvergence analysis

Keeping learning stable when workers fail

11

In distributed learning, some machines may crash or behave badly; these papers develop ways to keep training working reliably even when things go wrong.

ICLR 2026

Asynchronous Matching with Dynamic Sampling for Multimodal Dataset Distillation

Problem. Multimodal dataset distillation faces asynchronous optimization dynamics between modalities and lack of discrete class guidance for distribution coverage.
Approach. AMD framework enables asynchronous trajectory matching by decoupling image and text trajectory starting points, plus Semantics-Aware Prototype Mining leveraging clustering.
Contribution. Achieves gains of 4.5% IR@1, 9.6% IR@5, and 10.9% IR@10 on Flickr30k 200 pairs with negligible computational overhead.
dataset distillationtrajectory matchingprototype mining
ICLR 2026

Single-Loop Byzantine-Resilient Federated Bilevel Optimization

Problem. Federated bilevel optimization is susceptible to Byzantine failures but existing resilient approaches are restricted to single-level or use inefficient sub-loop updates.
Approach. BR-FedBi operates in single-loop with auxiliary variable for efficient hypergradient estimation, extended with momentum and probabilistic gradient estimator (PAGE).
Contribution. Achieves optimal Byzantine resilience and optimal sample complexity with single-loop efficiency.

Byzantine-Robust Federated Learning with Learnable Aggregation Weights

Problem. Federated learning with Byzantine clients and heterogeneous data lacks robust defense mechanisms that jointly optimize aggregation weights and model parameters.
Approach. Treats aggregation weights as learnable parameters jointly optimized with model parameters via alternating minimization, incorporating adaptive weighting into the FL objective.
Contribution. Consistently outperforms existing Byzantine-robust FL methods, particularly with highly heterogeneous data and large proportions of malicious clients.

ICML 2026

Mitigating Staleness in Asynchronous Pipeline Parallelism via Basis Rotation

Problem. Asynchronous pipeline parallelism suffers from gradient staleness that scales linearly with pipeline depth due to misalignment between the Hessian eigenbasis and standard coordinate basis.
Approach. Proposes basis rotation that aligns the optimizer's coordinate system with the Hessian eigenbasis, reducing oscillations in update trajectories and keeping delayed updates useful.
Contribution. Theoretically demonstrates that basis rotation minimizes basis misalignment and empirically shows reduced iterations needed for LLM training up to 3B parameters.

Beyond Binary: Continuous State Optimization with Graph-Structured Objectives

Problem. Large-scale learning systems must balance competing continuous objectives (fairness thresholds, diversity mixing rates) while minimizing system instability; existing frameworks only handle binary states.

Contribution. Reduces movement costs by over a factor of three in heterogeneous systems while maintaining similar cumulative losses, enabling efficient multi-objective optimization with system stability.

Contribution. Shows clipping removes dependence on maximum delay in oracle complexity, with convergence in expectation and high-probability convergence guarantees in asynchronous optimization.

Making optimization algorithms faster

17

Standard gradient descent can be slow; these papers adapt proven fast techniques like acceleration, momentum, and second-order methods to distributed settings.

ICLR 2026

Problem. Decentralized nonconvex stochastic optimization under heavy-tailed noise lacks theoretical guarantees, particularly for realistic gradient noise in attention models.
Approach. GT-NSGDm combines gradient tracking, normalization, and momentum to handle heavy-tailed noise on distributed nodes, guaranteeing optimal convergence rate.

Problem. Gradient descent for training PINNs suffers from poor convergence dependence on sample size and Gram matrix eigenvalues, leading to slow training.
Approach. Shows NGD enables maximal learning rates of O(1) independent of Gram matrix properties; demonstrates quadratic convergence for smooth activation functions via positive definiteness of general Gram matrices.
Contribution. Provides convergence analysis of natural gradient descent for two-layer PINNs, showing independence from Gram matrix conditioning and achieving improved convergence rates.

ICLR 2026

FZOO: Fast Zeroth-Order Optimizer for Fine‑Tuning Large Language Models towards Adam‑Scale Speed

Problem. Zeroth-order optimizers avoid memory bottlenecks of first-order optimizers like Adam but require tens of times more convergence steps, creating a fundamental speed-memory trade-off.
Approach. Introduces FZOO, which reduces convergence steps via batched one-sided estimates with adaptive step-sizes based on loss standard deviation, and accelerates per-batch computation using Rademacher random perturbations.
Contribution. On average outperforms MeZO by +3% accuracy while requiring 3x fewer forward passes; for RoBERTa-large achieves +5.6% improvement and 18x reduction in forward passes, reaching Adam-scale convergence speed.

Implicit Bias and Loss of Plasticity in Matrix Completion: Depth Promotes Low-Rankness

Learning with multiple objectives or constraints

11

Real problems often have multiple goals to optimize at once or constraints to satisfy; these papers extend distributed methods to handle such complex scenarios.

ICLR 2026

Convergence of Regret Matching in Potential Games and Constrained Optimization

Problem. Whether regret matching converges to Nash equilibria in potential games has been an open problem for two decades, and its performance in constrained optimization remains unclear.
Approach. The paper proves that alternating RM+ converges to an epsilon-KKT point after O_epsilon(1/epsilon^4) iterations by relating the KKT gap to accumulated regret, and establishes a lower bound showing RM can take exponential iterations.
Contribution. First proof that RM+ is a sound first-order optimizer with O_epsilon(1/epsilon^2) convergence when regrets are bounded, and a matching lower bound for RM.
regret matchingsaddle-point optimizationkkt analysis
ICLR 2026

Minimax-Optimal Aggregation for Density Ratio Estimation

Problem. Density ratio estimation methods are highly sensitive to hyperparameter selection, with suboptimal choices leading to poor convergence and empirical performance.
Approach. Proposes a novel model aggregation algorithm that trains multiple models with different hyperparameters and aggregates them, achieving minimax-optimal convergence without prior smoothness knowledge.
Contribution. Achieves minimax-optimal error convergence, surpassing cross-validation and model averaging baselines on standard benchmarks and large-scale domain adaptation tasks.
model aggregationdensity ratio estimationhyperparameter selection
ICLR 2026

On the $O(1/T)$ Convergence of Alternating Gradient Descent–Ascent in Bilinear Games

Problem. Alternating gradient descent-ascent (AltGDA) shows better empirical performance than simultaneous methods but lacks theoretical understanding, especially in constrained settings.
Approach. Proves AltGDA converges at O(1/T) ergodic rate with small constant stepsize for games with interior Nash equilibrium; develops PEP framework for joint optimization.
Contribution. First result showing alternation improves over simultaneous counterpart in constrained setting; PEP indicates AltGDA achieves O(1/T) vs O(1/√T) for simultaneous methods.

Asymmetric Perturbation in Solving Bilinear Saddle-Point Optimization

Problem. Symmetric perturbation in bilinear saddle-point optimization requires decreasing perturbation strength for convergence, resulting in slow convergence rates.

Second-Order Bilevel Optimization with Accelerated Convergence Rates

Problem. Decentralized learning performs poorly under high data heterogeneity and limited communication because peer-to-peer synchronization strategies are not well optimized for generalization.
Approach. The paper studies how to schedule communication over time in decentralized training, finding that concentrating communication in later stages and performing a single global merge at the final step surprisingly improves generalization.
Contribution. Showed theoretically that a fully connected final merge in decentralized SGD can match parallel SGD convergence rates, reinterpreting model discrepancies as beneficial rather than detrimental.
decentralized learningmodel mergingcommunication scheduling

ICML 2026

Thinned Mean Field Langevin Dynamics

Problem. Simulating mean-field Langevin dynamics for entropy-regularized objectives over probability distributions requires impractical O(N²) computational complexity due to all-pairs particle interactions.
Approach. KT-MFLD applies kernel thinning to reduce each particle's interaction set to a coreset of size O(N^½), enabling an O(N^3/2) approximation that maintains convergence guarantees through a theoretical analysis of the sparse interaction structure.
Contribution. Reduces computational complexity from O(N²) to O(N^3/2) while preserving the same convergence guarantees up to logarithmic factors, demonstrated on neural network training, quantization, and post-Bayesian inference tasks.
kernel thinningmean-field langevin dynamicscoreset approximation

Finding the sweet spot in model tuning 43

Modern large models need fine-tuning to new tasks, but this is expensive. Papers ask: how can you adapt models with fewer computations and memory without losing quality?

Approach. To address this question in a principled manner, we begin by introducing a continuous-time model of SMD as a stochastic differential equation (SDE) driven by a centered Lévy noise process with finite $p$-th order moments, $1 < p \leq 2$.

Local Entropy Search over Descent Sequences for Bayesian Optimization

Symmetry-Aware Bayesian Optimization via Max Kernels

Problem. Exploiting symmetries in Bayesian Optimization is limited because the max kernel, which captures maximum similarity across group orbits, is not positive semidefinite.
Approach. The paper revisits the max kernel idea by using a positive semidefinite projection of the max kernel to capture symmetries in expensive black-box function optimization.
Contribution. The projected max kernel achieves significantly lower regret on synthetic and real-world BO benchmarks without increasing computational complexity compared to existing invariant and non-invariant kernels.
bayesian optimizationkernel methodssymmetry exploitationgroup theory
ICML 2026

Meta-Black-Box Optimization Can Do Search Guidance for Expensive Constrained Multi-Objective Optimization

Problem. Existing Meta-Black-Box Optimization focuses on how to search but overlooks where to search in expensive constrained multi-objective optimization.
Approach. MetaSG-SAEA uses Max-Min Constraint-Calibrated Inequality for problem-agnostic region abstraction, diffusion-based population initialization, and attention-based state representation.
Contribution. Outperforms baselines across diverse benchmarks and generalizes across varying problem dimensions, population sizes, and numbers of objectives.

ICML 2026

Romberg-Extrapolated Zeroth-Order Gradient Estimator: Higher-Order Bias Reduction with Preserved Leading Directional Variance

Problem. Standard zeroth-order gradient estimators suffer from O(r^2) truncation bias at smoothing radius r and bias-reduction schemes typically increase variance.
Approach. Romberg-ZOGE forms Romberg-extrapolated linear combinations at radii {r/2^k} with reused perturbation direction, achieving O(r^{2R+2}) bias while preserving leading variance.
Contribution. Demonstrates faster and more stable zeroth-order SGD on synthetic benchmarks, simulator-based wireless optimization, and black-box prompt tuning.

Contribution. Demonstrate strong and robust performance on standard benchmarks and real-world surrogates across diverse settings.

Problem. Gaussian-process surrogates in Bayesian optimization become prohibitively expensive as observations scale to thousands or more, since hyperparameter fitting scales as O(N^3) and repeats at every iteration.

Zeroth-Order Optimization at the Edge of Stability

Balancing competing goals 26

Real problems have multiple objectives: accuracy, fairness, cost, speed. How do optimization algorithms find tradeoffs instead of optimizing just one goal?

ICLR 2026

DR-Submodular Maximization with Stochastic Biased Gradients: Classical and Quantum Gradient Algorithms

Problem. DR-submodular maximization with realistic stochastic biased gradients remains challenging, and existing methods assume unbiased gradient noise.
Approach. Generalizes the Lyapunov framework to biased stochastic gradients, considers convex sets with largest element constraints, and proposes both classical zero-order algorithms with O(ε^-3) and quantum algorithms with O(ε^-1) iteration complexity.
Contribution. Provides 1/e approximation algorithm for non-monotone DR-submodular maximization under novel constraints, surpassing the hardness result 1/4 for general convex constraints, with quantum acceleration validated numerically.
submodular optimizationgradient estimationquantum algorithmsconstrained optimization
ICLR 2026

Reducing Contextual Stochastic Bilevel Optimization via Structured Function Approximation

Problem. Contextual Stochastic Bilevel Optimization requires solving distinct lower-level problems for each context, resulting in prohibitive sample and computational complexity.
Approach. A reduction framework approximates lower-level solutions using expressive basis functions like Chebyshev polynomials, transforming CSBO into standard SBO solvable with joint samples.
Contribution. Achieves ε-stationary solution with near-optimal complexity of Õ(ε⁻³), matching best-known rate for standard SBO, with strong performance on inverse and hyperparameter optimization.
bilevel optimizationbasis functionsapproximation theory

Contribution. SPREAD matches or exceeds leading baselines in efficiency, scalability, and Pareto front coverage across multi-objective optimization benchmarks.

Problem. Deep networks trained on nonstationary data must balance stability (retaining prior knowledge) and plasticity (adapting to new tasks), but standard reinitialization methods are difficult to tune.
Approach. FIRE proposes principled reinitialization by quantifying stability through Squared Frobenius Error and plasticity through Deviation from Isometry, solving a constrained optimization problem minimized via Newton-Schulz iteration.
Contribution. FIRE consistently outperforms naive training and standard reinitialization across continual vision learning, language modeling, and reinforcement learning domains.

Understanding when and why optimization works 32

Gradient descent and neural network training have surprising behaviors. What are the mathematical principles and limits? Why does noise sometimes help? When do algorithms get stuck?

Consistent Low-Rank Approximation

Problem. Maintaining consistent low-rank approximations of sequentially arriving data while minimizing solution changes is a challenging online optimization problem.
Approach. The paper develops algorithms that provide sequences of rank-k subspaces approximating optimal low-rank decompositions with bounded recourse, showing upper and lower bounds for both additive and multiplicative approximation guarantees.
Contribution. The paper proves recourse bounds ranging from O(k/ε log(nd)) for additive approximation to improved bounds of k^(3/2)/ε² poly log(nd) for multiplicative approximation on integer matrices.

Approach. Logits-SAM applies Sharpness-Aware Minimization only to the output layer as a computationally efficient variant that suppresses curvature expansion and regularizes coordinate-wise dynamics in logit space.
Contribution. Logits-SAM consistently improves DPO effectiveness on Pythia-2.8B and Mistral-7B across multiple datasets with negligible computational overhead.

Minor First, Major Last: A Depth-Induced Implicit Bias of Sharpness-Aware Minimization

Approach. Analyze SAM on linearly separable binary classification using linear diagonal networks, studying how different SAM variants and initialization affect the limit direction of learned features.
Contribution. For L=2 networks, ℓ∞-SAM converges to unpredictable limit directions dependent on initialization, while ℓ2-SAM exhibits 'sequential feature discovery' where minor coordinates initially dominate then shift to major ones as training progresses.

Moving data across spaces and distributions 41

Sometimes data lives in different spaces (high-dimensional to low-dimensional, one distribution to another). How do you transport or transform it efficiently while preserving structure?

ICLR 2026

Random-projection ensemble dimension reduction

Problem. High-dimensional regression requires effective dimension reduction, but existing approaches may not adaptively select optimal projection directions.
Approach. Uses ensemble of random projections with disjoint groups, selects best projection per group by empirical performance, and aggregates via SVD.

Complexity Analysis of Normalizing Constant Estimation: from Jarzynski Equality to Annealed Importance Sampling and beyond

Approach. The paper provides non-asymptotic complexity analysis of annealed importance sampling using Girsanov theorem and optimal transport, and proposes a reverse diffusion sampler algorithm to handle the large action problem in geometric interpolation.

Achieving Structurally Robust Gromov Wasserstein Distance via Adaptive Dual-Mask

Problem. The Gromov-Wasserstein distance is fragile to structural noise because it relies on global quadratic coupling and existing robust extensions only address node-centric outliers while neglecting edge-induced distortions from spurious connections.
Approach. Propose the Structurally Robust Gromov-Wasserstein (SRGW) distance with a structure-aware dual-mask mechanism that adaptively filters both node and edge-induced geometric distortions during optimization, solved via a Mask-Guided GW Algorithm.
Contribution. The algorithm converges to a critical point under the Kurdyka-Łojasiewicz framework and achieves superior alignment quality on synthetic and real-world subgraph alignment benchmarks under severe structural noise.

Incorporating Importance Weighting in Optimal Transport Based Domain Alignment

Problem. Optimal transport-based domain adaptation bounds rely on incomputable entanglement terms, making optimization impractical for real distribution shifts.
Approach. The work introduces gradual shift and probabilistic margin assumptions to control the entanglement term, then tightens the bound via importance weighting to derive the weighted Wasserstein regularized risk, which is equivalent to an unbalanced OT problem.
Contribution. The improved weighted bound converges to a nearest-neighbor alignment strategy and shows better performance than prior work across multiple distribution shifts.

Approach. SoftJAX and SoftTorch provide soft differentiable replacements for hard operations including elementwise operators, Boolean utilities, and axis-wise operators based on optimal transport or permutahedron projections.

Approach. AvAtar quantifies informativeness by measuring gradient-based impact on global alignment results via gradient propagation through entropy-regularized OT, solved efficiently using the adjoint-state method and conjugate gradient descent.

Problem. Existing gradient-based sufficient dimension reduction estimators either suffer from curse of dimensionality in ambient space or incur quadratic per-iteration cost when localizing to reduced space.
Approach. SMAVE recasts MAVE risk as smooth maximization on Stiefel manifold with closed-form Riemannian gradient, combining sparse projected-space nearest-neighbor localization with Riemannian stochastic gradient ascent.
Contribution. Almost-sure convergence with non-asymptotic rate matching standard non-convex first-order scaling; matches or improves synthetic subspace recovery at high dimension and uniformly outperforms OPG with orders of magnitude lower runtime.
riemannian optimizationsufficient dimension reductionstiefel manifoldgradient descent
ICML 2026

Fast Spectrally Sparse Signal Reconstruction via Jacobi-Preconditioned Gradient Descent

Problem. Low-rank Hankel matrix completion requires balancing first-order algorithmic efficiency with fast convergence independent of condition number.
Approach. Uses Jacobi-preconditioned gradient descent with a generator mapping factor-based iterates to matrix space, establishing equivalence with manifold-based methods.
Contribution. Achieves linear convergence independent of condition number while maintaining low per-iteration complexity across broad problem settings.
Jacobi preconditioningHankel matrix completionmanifold methods
ICML 2026

Riemannian Neural Optimal Transport

Problem. Extending neural optimal transport to high-dimensional Riemannian manifolds is challenged by curse of dimensionality: discrete approximations require exponentially more parameters with manifold dimension.
Approach. Riemannian Neural OT (RNOT) uses continuous neural-network parameterizations incorporating geometric structure by construction while avoiding discretization.
Contribution. Approximates Riemannian OT maps with sub-exponential complexity in dimension and demonstrates improved scalability relative to discretization-based baselines.
optimal transportriemannian geometryneural networks
ICML 2026

Streaming Sliced Optimal Transport

Problem. Computing sliced Wasserstein distance from streaming samples is computationally challenging while maintaining statistical and memory scalability.
Approach. Stream-SW introduces a streaming estimator of one-dimensional Wasserstein via quantile approximation techniques, applied across all projections.
Contribution. Stream-SW achieves more accurate approximation of SW than random subsampling with lower memory consumption on Gaussian and mixture distributions and diverse applications.

ICML 2026

Rethinking the Flow-based Gradual Domain Adaptation: A Semi-Dual Optimal Transport Perspective

Problem. Flow-based gradual domain adaptation relies on sample-based log-likelihood estimation which discards useful information and degrades performance when intermediate domains must be synthesized.
Approach. Proposes Entropy-regularized Semi-dual Unbalanced Optimal Transport (E-SUOT) that reformulates flow-based GDA as a Lagrangian dual problem with entropy regularization, converting unstable min-max training into stable sequential optimization.
Contribution. Provides theoretical analysis of stability and generalization, with extensive experiments demonstrating the efficacy of the E-SUOT framework for constructing intermediate domains.
optimal transportentropy regularizationdomain adaptation
ICML 2026

Riemannian Dueling Optimization

Problem. Dueling optimization (using only comparison oracles) has been studied mainly in unconstrained Euclidean spaces, leaving important applications on Riemannian manifolds unsolved.
Approach. Proposes Riemannian Dueling Normalized Gradient Descent (RDNGD) and a projection-free Riemannian Dueling Frank–Wolfe (RDFW) method, establishing iteration complexity bounds for geodesically smooth or convex objectives.
Contribution. Extends dueling optimization to Riemannian manifolds with theoretical iteration and oracle complexity guarantees, validated on synthetic and real applications.

Convex Distance Operator Transport: A Convex and Geometry-Preserving Formulation

Problem. Optimal transport methods struggle to align distributions across heterogeneous domains while preserving geometric structure and remain computationally intractable in many settings.
Approach. Convex Distance Operator Transport (CDOT) uses operator-based regularization that aligns aggregated distance structures via distance and conditional expectation operators while maintaining convexity and valid pseudometric properties.
Contribution. Achieves convexity while preserving geometry on attributed compact metric-measure spaces; derives non-asymptotic risk bounds and demonstrates better performance on point clouds, brain connectomes, and graph classification.

ICML 2026

Muon in Associative Memory Learning: Training Dynamics and Scaling Laws

Problem. Muon matrix sign gradient updates show empirical gains but lack theoretical understanding of dynamics and scaling behavior.
Approach. Studies Muon in linear associative memory with softmax retrieval and hierarchical frequency spectrum; shows Muon mitigates gradient descent's frequency imbalance via implicit preconditioner.
Contribution. Proves exponential speedup in noiseless case and derives scaling law for noisy power-law spectrum; validates through synthetic and LLaMA pretraining.

ICML 2026

Spectra: Rethinking Optimizers for LLMs Under Spectral Anisotropy

Problem. Gradient signals in LLM training are highly anisotropic with linguistic structure concentrating energy in dominant directions while context information resides in tail, suppressing tail learning.
Approach. Spectra tracks spike subspace via cached warm-started power iteration and applies low-rank spectral shaping; suppresses dominant spike without amplifying noise-sensitive tail.
Contribution. Across Qwen3 and LLaMA3 models, achieves lowest final validation loss and improves downstream accuracy by 1.41/0.89 and 1.62/0.66 points over AdamW/Muon; reaches matched loss targets up to 1.31× faster.

ICML 2026

Delving into Muon and Beyond: Deep Analysis and Extensions

Problem. The Muon optimizer's underlying mechanisms and relationship to adaptive optimizers like Adam remain insufficiently understood.
Approach. View Muon as the p=0 endpoint of spectral transformations with variants (p=0.5, 0.25, 1); develop efficient computation via coupled Newton iteration avoiding explicit SVD.
Contribution. Reveals Muon as effective spectral normalization but not universally superior to Adam; RMS-normalized updates outperform first-moment updates for stable optimization.

Making predictions that know their limits 19

ML models should admit when they're uncertain. How do you quantify confidence, calibrate predictions, or provide guarantees that hold even on new data?

ICLR 2026

Adaptive Conformal Guidance for Learning under Uncertainty

Problem. Guidance signals from annotated data, pseudo-labels, or expert demonstrations can be noisy due to domain shifts or limited availability, causing degraded performance when blindly trusted.
Approach. Adaptive Conformal Guidance (AdaConG) dynamically modulates the influence of guidance signals based on their associated uncertainty quantified via split conformal prediction.
Contribution. AdaConG improves performance and robustness under imperfect guidance; in gridworld navigation it accelerates convergence and achieves over 6× higher rewards than the best baseline.
conformal predictionuncertainty quantificationadaptive weighting
ICLR 2026

Measuring Uncertainty Calibration

Problem. Estimating calibration error of binary classifiers from finite datasets requires practical, non-asymptotic methods without restrictive distributional assumptions.
Approach. The paper provides upper bounds for classifiers with bounded-variation calibration functions and a method to modify any classifier so its calibration error is efficiently upper-bounded without impacting performance.
Contribution. The paper provides non-asymptotic, distribution-free methods with practical procedures for measuring calibration error on real-world datasets.
calibration error boundsclassifier modificationbounded-variation analysis
ICLR 2026

Prior-free Tabular Test-time Adaptation

Problem. DNNs suffer severe performance degradation on tabular data under distribution shift, and prior test-time adaptation methods designed for images fail on tabular modality and often depend on source domain access.
Approach. PFT3A addresses label and feature shift without source access through: (1) class prior estimation for calibration, (2) robust feature learning via alignment of source-like and target-like features.
Contribution. PFT3A enables prior-free tabular test-time adaptation addressing both label and feature shifts without requiring source domain or prior knowledge.
class prior estimationfeature alignmentdistribution shift adaptation
ICLR 2026

Non-Asymptotic Analysis of Efficiency in Conformalized Regression

Problem. Conformal prediction provides coverage guarantees but efficiency depends on prediction set size; prior work treats miscoverage level as fixed constant without analyzing sample complexity.
Approach. Establish non-asymptotic bounds on deviation of prediction set length from oracle interval for conformalized quantile and median regression trained via SGD.
Contribution. Bounds of order O(1/sqrt(n) + 1/(alpha^2*n) + 1/sqrt(m) + exp(-alpha^2*m)) reveal phase transitions in convergence rates across regimes.
conformal predictionquantile regressionstatistical learning
ICLR 2026

Singleton-Optimized Conformal Prediction

Problem. Conformal prediction often produces large prediction sets even when a singleton (single label) would be optimal, and existing efficiency methods primarily optimize average set size.
Approach. Non-conformity score motivated by minimizing non-singleton probability via convex-geometric reformulation, computed in O(K) time for K-class problems.
Contribution. SOCOP increases singleton frequency by over 20% compared to standard scores with minimal impact on average set size in image classification and LLM multiple-choice tasks.
conformal predictionnon-conformity scoringconvex-geometric optimization
ICLR 2026

Distributional Consistency Loss: Beyond Pointwise Data Terms in Inverse Problems

Problem. Conventional data-fidelity loss functions seek pointwise agreement with noisy measurements, leading to overfitting to noise in inverse problems.
Approach. Distributional Consistency loss evaluates data-fidelity collectively by testing whether observed measurements are statistically consistent with noise distributions implied by the current estimate.
Contribution. DC loss acts as a direct plug-in replacement for standard data consistency terms, avoiding overfitting to measurement noise without early stopping.
distributional consistencyinverse problemsstatistical calibration
ICLR 2026

COMPASS: Robust Feature Conformal Prediction for Medical Segmentation Metrics

Problem. Conformal prediction applied naively to segmentation model outputs is inefficient because it treats the complex, non-linear segmentation-to-metric pipeline as a black box.
Approach. COMPASS performs calibration directly in the model's representation space by perturbing intermediate features along low-dimensional subspaces maximally sensitive to the target metric.
Contribution. Produces significantly tighter conformal prediction intervals than traditional baselines on four medical image segmentation tasks for area estimation of skin lesions and anatomical structures while maintaining valid marginal coverage.

Problem. Predict-then-optimize approaches often sideline human judgment, creating disconnects between algorithmic outputs and expert intuition that undermines trust in high-stakes decisions.
Approach. Propose CREDO framework that provides distribution-free upper bounds on suboptimality probability for any expert-proposed decision using inverse optimization and conformal prediction.

ICLR 2026

Hinge Regression Tree: A Newton Method for Oblique Regression Tree Splitting

Problem. Learning high-quality oblique tree splits is NP-hard and practical methods rely on slow search or theory-free heuristics, limiting transparency-power tradeoff.
Approach. Hinge Regression Tree reframes each split as non-linear least-squares over two linear predictors with max/min envelope, using alternating fitting equivalent to damped Newton method.
Contribution. Achieves fast, stable convergence with universal approximator status and O(δ²) approximation rate, matching or outperforming baselines with more compact structures.

Contribution. Adapts to unknown discount factor; aggregation via Discounted-Normal-Predictor combines decisions of experts with different discount factors.
online convex optimizationdiscounted regretexpert aggregation

PAS: Estimating the target accuracy before domain adaptation

Problem. Selecting the best source domain and pre-trained model for domain adaptation is non-trivial due to lack of labeled validation data for the target domain and the large number of available pre-trained models.

Contribution. PAS correlates strongly with actual target accuracy and consistently guides selection of the best-performing pre-trained model and source domain for adaptation.

Problem. Learning-to-rank via probabilistic permutation modeling is intractable due to factorial growth of permutation space, yet full-order structure is important for capturing ranking relationships.
Approach. Introducing Full-Order Bound (FOB), a tractable lower bound on observed ordering probability that factorizes across items while preserving order structure, solved via safe-region gradient ascent.
Contribution. FOB improves full-list ordering metrics and remains competitive on NDCG on large-scale learning-to-rank benchmarks.

Problem. We consider max-min and min-max problems with objective functions that are possibly non-smooth, submodular with respect to the minimiser and concave with respect to the maximiser.
Approach. We investigate the performance of a zeroth-order method applied to this problem.
Contribution. The method is based on the subgradient of the Lovász extension of the objective function with respect to the minimiser and based on Gaussian smoothing to estimate the smoothed function gradient with respect to the maximiser.

Steady-State Behavior of Constant-Stepsize Stochastic Approximation: Gaussian Approximation and Tail Bounds

Problem. Fixed-stepsize stochastic approximation produces a stationary distribution that is hard to analyze, and existing asymptotic theory provides no usable error bounds for finite stepsizes.
Approach. The paper derives explicit non-asymptotic error bounds by analyzing the steady-state distribution through Stein's method and moment bounds, studying stochastic gradient descent, linear SA, and contractive nonlinear SA under both i.i.d. and Markovian noise.
Contribution. Dimension- and stepsize-dependent Wasserstein bounds between centered-scaled steady state and its Gaussian limit, plus sharp tail control that decays as stepsize shrinks.

An Exterior Method for Nonnegative Matrix Factorization

Problem. Interior methods for nonnegative matrix factorization enforce nonnegativity constraints throughout optimization, which can impede progress in the nonconvex landscape and lead to slow convergence or suboptimal stationary points.
Approach. An exterior framework (eNMF) separates low-rank approximation from nonnegativity enforcement by initializing from optimal unconstrained factorization and using a rotation procedure to map factors to an exterior point closest to the nonnegative orthant.
Contribution. Consistently outperforms 81 competitor configurations achieving up to 30% lower reconstruction error under equal-time settings and up to 150% speedup, with 99% of experiments converging to equivalent factor matrices.

OptMaster: A DAG-Based Framework for Formulation and Heuristic Discovery in Optimization

Problem. LLMs struggle to automate optimization by bridging the gap between real-world requirements and mathematical formulations or effective heuristic designs, especially for NP-hard problems.
Approach. OptMaster structures optimization as a DAG where each node represents a candidate solution, enabling cross-branch knowledge transfer; it replaces textual self-reflection with verification code to suppress hallucinations and ground evaluation in deterministic computation.
